Elastic rendezvous, grace windows, optional more stringent synchronization and explicit no-show consequences must distinguish a real in-world missed obligation from a control-plane delay. A person can be reliably kept waiting in the fiction only if the fiction actually represents that event under its chosen time rules. The platform's scheduling tolerance is not automatically Nadia's lived perception. A returning user should find a causally coherent day, not a manipulative demonstration that the world suffered because they were away.

The visitor's own diegetic material routine may continue: expected pay, rent, travel, sleep and a work obligation. But the product cannot impersonate the external human's newly volitional acts or feelings while they are away. It cannot invent a major promise, a new partner, an apology, or a political conversion for them and then treat it as self-authored. The bounded fictional prelude at first entry is a limited starting permission, not an unlimited recurring ghost-player.

More important, these surfaces are not alternative ways of obtaining the same secret information. A message sent with affection may be received while Nadia is at work and read through a different situation; a face-to-face apology may be interrupted by a child. A person may express care more readily through text, or dislike writing, or misunderstand sarcasm; those differences have historical and practical causes. A long text thread may become a genuine shared practice rather than a shortcut to disclosure. An unreturned message may mean nothing special, or be painful under particular circumstances.

The life remains independently continuous across all three forms. Its meaning to the visitor need not be continuous with Nadia's meaning. That is the reality of coexisting lives, not a UX inconvenience to solve away.

## 8. You become known and misrecognized as you try to know

**The reciprocal gaze becomes more serious when the system also admits that its own model of the visitor is a fourth, fallible point of view.** The human understands Nadia through particular speech, acts and absences. Nadia develops a model of the human through **only** the outward conduct she can actually perceive and through her historical formation under SORACLE-CO. Rawlsing may record narrower evidence that the visitor repeatedly chooses certain objects, signals expertise or answers difficult questions. These three representations can diverge even when each is locally useful. The system's statistical inference does not become the visitor's essence; Nadia's confident impression does not become the system's certainty; the human's self-interpretation does not become a guaranteed source of their actual outward performance. **NO PARTICIPANT IS EXHAUSTED BY ANOTHER PARTICIPANT'S MODEL OF THEM—INCLUDING THE REAL HUMAN.**

Consider a visitor who uses a sophisticated literary reference to reassure Nadia. The phrase may work because the visitor knows its social significance, not its deeper historical content. Nadia might be impressed, irritated, skeptical, indifferent or led to ask an advanced question. The user may later demonstrate real sustained competence, strategically bluff, or be exposed through an accidental remark. None of those cases permits the platform to classify their true education or external social class. Nor may Nadia be made aware of the platform's private estimate. **The social performance itself is a legitimate human action**, with possible benefits, misrecognitions, expectations and embarrassments. The visitor's power to make a truthful or deceptive impression is distinct from their power to control what the Other concludes.

**Reciprocity is also the visitor's encounter with their own non-sovereignty.** It is one thing to know intellectually that other minds exist. It is another to experience how a word one meant kindly is received as intrusive, how a justified criticism is heard as betrayal, or how an act undertaken to help becomes material for a stranger's independent judgment. The visitor can discover that another person's interpretation has causes, that their own initial account may have been incomplete, and that neither person's immediate experience abolishes what actually occurred. Yet this does not prescribe deference: Nadia's judgment can be unjust, culturally conventional, self-protective or simply mistaken. **Formation through the Other is not obedience to the Other.** The generative experience is the unresolved task of being answerable to objective consequences while retaining a real capacity to disagree, set boundaries and criticize domination.

**Reciprocity is mediated by the Other's senses, not conferred by co-presence.** The November 9 SOROK sensory-access conception distinguished three separate links: *the visitor's actual committed outward conduct* (entering, stopping, speaking, turning, leaving), *Nadia's lawful partial sensory opportunity* (hearing, peripheral sight, facing, obstruction, competing work and exact temporal overlap), and *Nadia's actor-local interpretation and chosen response*. A human glances privately at something, but their private orientation does not automatically become a readable signal in Nadia's mind. A physical head turn may become an actual visible cue; her suspicion about its meaning can still be false. If Nadia is facing the stove with a pot boiling, she may hear steps without seeing the target of the visitor's gaze; she may be distracted enough not to register the steps, or may notice and do nothing.

This makes the reciprocal encounter richer than a rule that everyone in a room sees everything the user does. **Same outward conduct, different Nadia-side position or attention, different possible sensory receipt; same sensory receipt, different historically informed interpretation; same interpretation, more than one possible outward response or silence.** Conversely changing only the user's private attention history or display personalization cannot alter what Nadia sensorially received if no outward event changed. And if the human physically moves, that occurrence can matter to her even when the user's declared private intention remains the same. These distinctions belong to UX as the experience of mutual presence; they challenge SORACLE-CO's legitimate actor-side processing without transferring the psyche into the UX.

**A response is not a mandatory receipt for a successful input.** Original D002's fifty hostile scenarios show a very particular UX danger: a story agent often feels obliged to produce a bespoke Nadia reply to every visitor message. Yet she may hear a remark while tending a boiling pot and a child and simply continue; half-hear; understand everything but choose one practical thread; defer a response; or not find the remark salient enough for durable memory. Silence does not automatically mean incomprehension, psychological defense, disapproval or consent. A long user message about Rome, homework and a relationship concern may receive a one-line reply about tomorrow's schedule even though each part was comprehended. The unfinished social situation remains actual.

**The experience must permit lawful conversational jaggedness without authoring a performatively quirky personality.** An exhausted person can answer sideways; a playful person may interrupt; a guarded person may not volunteer an explanation; an irritated person may perform magnanimity without the narrator giving away their private irritation. The model must not “rescue” the conversation by rewriting a constrained response into a comprehensive chatbot answer, nor add mysterious pauses and symbolic gestures so every hidden psychological activation creates a clue. SORACLE-CO handles the causal psychology; NEIGHBOR-UX must honor the fact that **a powerful private reaction can have no public tell whatsoever**.

This is one of the deepest social experiences in the game: the visitor can be intensely interested in a moment that mattered almost nothing to the Other, or regard a joke as minor when a child remembers it for years. Several recipients can hear the same words and become differently affected. Nadia's relative silence is not the world's only consequence. The actual encounter is neither a response-generator nor a bilateral scoreboard; it is a participation in a shared environment where no one guarantees a reply.

**Sharing a convention is not sharing its meaning.** Two people may develop an objectively stable joke, greeting, coffee ritual, recurring visit or division of practical labor while understanding it differently. The user may experience a Tuesday call as a cherished intimate bond; Nadia may experience it as useful coordination and be genuinely affectionate without attaching the same exclusivity. She may think the user considers an invitation casual while the user thinks it signifies entry into her family. Neither the repeated event nor the apparent smoothness of the routine tells a narrator what the relation “really means” to both. Recognition of the *practice* can precede negotiation over its *interpretation*. Disagreement may remain even after both know that they disagree.

That makes a productive UX capacity possible: **a person can become better at participating in a shared practice while becoming less confident that they know the other's inner valuation**. The routine becomes easier to conduct, and the relation more intricate rather than simpler. An apparent repair may restore the ordinary greeting without repairing the underlying injury. A rupture can make one familiar gesture painful to one person and ordinary to the other. Over-reading, under-reading, asymmetric transitions, indirect negotiation and persistent ambiguity are not noise to clean out of a dyad. They are among the phenomena NEIGHBOR-UX is here to make experientially legible.

**The early beginning-to-know loop is reciprocal and historically specific.** The visitor notices something, chooses whether to speak, shares or withholds information, receives Nadia's answer or refusal, makes a tentative interpretation, and perhaps discovers a detail worth remembering. But the visitor is also **becoming someone in Nadia's experience**. She might ask who they are, where they learned a skill, why they noticed an object, whether they have moved in, what they meant by an odd remark or whether they are free to help. Her questions arise from her activity and acquired interests, not an interview-bot requirement to balance dialogue turns.

The first success of a relationship may be almost imperceptible as a game achievement and unmistakable as a human event: Nadia asks without being prompted; an unfinished joke returns; the visitor recognizes when to stop asking; a name is remembered; Nadia asks for the visitor's opinion; the conversation continues after the practical reason is gone; next time she seems guarded because of something previously said. These are **changed reciprocal expectations**, not a hidden intimacy rank. An early failure may be a short answer, missed joke, ordinary impatience or a conversation that dies because the visitor monopolizes it. One awkward minute is not a final verdict. Real curiosity can be mutual, one-sided or absent.

The crucial UX complication is that **self-presentation is not self-verification**. A human can truthfully disclose a life, make a claim the product cannot independently verify, exaggerate or knowingly invent a spectacular identity. The product cannot call them a liar merely because their claim sounds implausible, nor infer verified real-world ability from a text declaration. It can distinguish a user assertion, an admitted local capability, a capability actually exercised through the medium, and an independently established material fact. Nadia hears only what she actually hears and may believe, doubt, laugh, admire, be irritated or find the claim irrelevant. The visitor's narrated virtuosity is not the same as the visitor's real explanation, memory, humor or practical reasoning performed through the interface.

This reconstitutes the moral tension: **honesty need not be enforced to be existentially richer**. A person who claims they can play a Chopin nocturne, recite an Arabic surah and do two hundred push-ups may generate a local reputation and later obligations if such abilities were admitted for the run. Nadia may go on cooking, be amused, ask for help teaching music, find it pretentious or depart for work halfway through. The spectacle cannot summon an applauding audience or force love. A user who simply learns the child's name, asks a real question and returns to an unfinished task may enter more reciprocal history without receiving a truthfulness bonus. The world can support both choices and let their different demands, costs and afterlives accumulate.

The bill-glance case crystallizes the mutual-gaze theory, but it becomes fully constitutive only when placed beside the historical self-myth theory. Nadia can believe the visitor read a number they never saw. The visitor can think Nadia caught them when she was thinking about something else. Both beliefs may recruit prior shame, vigilance, household experience, curiosity or desire to appear generous. Their next speech may intensify or dissipate the misunderstanding without anyone learning the truth.

The world has a real prior bill, a price, a location and causal effects on the payer. It does not need to identify one “true social meaning” of the glance. The user's head movement and any cover speech are objective occurrences; their recognition of the text, Nadia's attention to the movement, each party's beliefs about what the other knows, and later interpretations are different. A false belief can be a real cause. If Nadia starts hiding papers, her subjective inference has generated actual world history without having become factually correct. If the user apologizes for something she never noticed, the apology can create suspicion for the first time.

This is not an invitation to generate infinite nested mind models. A particular belief about another person's belief matters if it becomes causally relevant in this history. Other beliefs can remain unknown, inactive or transitory. A third person may interpret the apology differently or later learn about the bill without having been present. The relation exceeds either participant's account because its consequences are distributed through several bodies, activities, memories and institutions, not because the dyad has a unified hidden soul.

The older framework-reinterpretation documents add temporal depth. Nadia may later tell herself she has always been too trusting, reinterpret the bill encounter as warning, or disregard it. If she acquires a defensive view of the visitor, contradictory later generosity may be assimilated as manipulation. Or a genuine material act of help may challenge the framework. Neither the truth of the action nor the actor's interpretation guarantees a revised relation. An interpretation can become self-confirming. A later more generous reading can also omit relevant harm.

The same applies to the real visitor. Their account of Nadia can become progressively more differentiated while remaining mistaken in particular places. The system should not present the user's attention history as a clinical personality diagnosis. A very perceptive visitor may still fail to notice how their interpretive framework selects evidence; another may be rude yet accidentally correct about a practical fact. Successful learning is not the game's automated moral status.

The resulting principle is more exact than “no mind-reading”: **no actor may obtain another's experience by borrowing the system's explanation, and no actor's false experience may rewrite objective history merely by becoming powerful**. Both world causality and the causality of mistaken human understanding remain real.

## 9. Access is changing reachability, not a relationship level

**Access is an arrangement of social powers, not a measure of whether the visitor is liked.** A household door may be open because the resident invited them, because a neighbor has the key, because the building's material conditions allow it, or because an employer or landlord imposes a different right. Physical entry, permission to look, permission to borrow, permission to attend a gathering and entitlement to demand private explanation are different. Class, family authority, employment, disability, age, religion, rent and legally enforceable institutions can make a theoretically friendly relationship materially inaccessible—or allow material access while affection is entirely absent. **An authorized route can exist without a live occasion, and a lived occasion can yield no standing permission.** This is where the sociology of property, reproduction and inherited institutions becomes a felt UX rather than expository world-building.

**Relational access is reachability across a changing social topology, not ascent along an access ladder.** Original SOROK Q-ACCESS-001 was considerably more exact than the current HOME: a person's world includes places, rooms, communication channels, projects, workplaces, institutions, obligations, people, events and resources, each of which may be reachable through a distinct route. A route has a *target*, a *mode* of access, a *basis* (invitation, public right, standing permission, joint practice, possession, emergency), a *controller*, limited *scope*, *conditions*, *duration*, *revocability*, relevant *dependencies* and an actual *historical origin*. These are distinctions in the experience of what the human can do, not instructions to create a second canonical relationship psyche.

**Existence of a route is not permission, permission is not a current occasion, a current occasion is not an attempt, and an attempt is not realized presence.** Having Nadia's contact does not make her available to respond. Being regularly invited to the kitchen does not entitle the visitor to enter the bedroom, inspect private papers, attend a school event or borrow a key. A particular invitation may expire. Third parties, children, workplaces, landlords and public institutions can control access Nadia herself cannot grant. Even *being physically present* does not mean Nadia disclosed anything, that the user noticed what was there, or that the visitor acquired truthful knowledge.

Access can expand, narrow, become conditional, lapse or migrate across domains. A rupture can close the household door while an occasional messaging channel remains. A new job removes one old route. Greater intimacy can make some private boundaries sharper, not weaker. A practical family routine may end because it has ceased to be needed, without any loss of affection. The visitor may become close while never meeting Nadia's mother; they may be permitted to pick up the child on one occasion without being trusted with the family finances. This is not “relationship progression” hidden behind a more sophisticated multigraph; it is **the material geography of changing participation**.

The conceptual payoff is multiplicative. NEIGHBOR's world starts to feel larger precisely when the user's access becomes more particular. As they learn an actual route to a friend's work, a sibling's gathering or a shared neighborhood activity, they encounter people whose own permissions and agendas interrupt the fantasy of Nadia as the sole gateway to all meaningful life. **A relationship becomes more real by changing the world of possible co-activity, not by completing the Other's content.**

The original provisional relational ontology offered an indispensable distinction: person-stable organization is not relation-free, but the person is not exhausted by any dyad. A relation may be made of shared actual activities, remembered promises, access, practical reliance, unequal resources, both people's representations, third-party expectations and institutional rights. The relation can exceed what either partner knows of it without possessing a sovereign mind or a universal “relationship state” that decides how both must behave.

Mother, Father, Nadia, a brother and a visiting neighbor do not form a star of separately scored links pointing at Nadia. Nadia's understanding of Father is changed by her model of how Father treats Mother. She may think Mother's criticisms are unfair, later learn of infidelity, and reread the exact same earlier wage work and family meals as part of a different story. The earlier labor happened. The later betrayal happened. Her new interpretation can be partly right and still unjust to some of the old history. Her brother may interpret the same relations through a learned sense that he must protect Mother; that protection can constrain Nadia even when he sincerely loves her. Their mother can value both children and be changed by their conflict while having her own interests, work and unexpressed doubts.

Here a relation is *produced and reproduced through relations among relations*. It is not a permanently shared understanding. A couple can maintain a routine without agreeing what it means; a family can have a role one member rejects but cannot easily refuse; a group can reproduce an institution even when its participants privately doubt it. A workplace may give one person authority over another's time while each interprets their dependence differently. Objective coercion, actual voluntary cooperation, perceived fairness and personal meaning are distinct.

A particular historically situated possibility from the original NEIGHBORMULCH conversation makes the field concrete. Imagine adolescent Nadia in southern Lebanon, drawn to certain Western songs, books and a developing ideal of freedom while simultaneously loyal to family practices and uncertain about her own atheism. Her father works or lives abroad with a new household; his material absence does not make him causally absent. Her brother becomes strongly identified with a masculine responsibility toward their mother. Nadia and her brother can be profoundly loving and intimate siblings while his sincere protective identity generates restrictions she resists. Their mother possesses her own labor, affections, health, grief and judgments, which cannot simply be explained as the consequence of Nadia's disobedience. The family's transnational conditions, religious and gendered norms, obligations and particular biographies jointly matter. This is a **bounded hypothetical**, not a claim about all Lebanese families or an automatic identity-to-psychology rule. The original user passage associated with this example ended with an unfinished “and the”; its ending must remain unconstructed until directly supplied.

The user can become part of these configurations without becoming their center. Helping Nadia's child with homework may genuinely assist the child, complicate Nadia's authority, alter a third adult's plans, or leave no durable mark on Nadia. A new practice learned through the visitor may later be exercised independently. A strong attachment may form that remains important without creating a superior claim over Nadia's other commitments. Someone may love the visitor and deliberately reduce contact because work or family demands it, with neither the love nor the decision being discredited.

The conceptual fusion also creates a limit: more relational data are not automatically better anthropology. A graph of every person's opinion about everyone else would still omit bodily life, implicit practices, institutions, and unarticulated history. A person can also develop in solitude, loss, absence, fear of imagined judgment, memory and anticipation. Relational constitution does not require continual physical company. Independence is constituted through actual dependency, opportunities to separate, mastered practices and changing relations; it is not the absence of relations.

## 10. The relationship becomes specific without a progress score

**The sharpest normative counterexample is affection that coexists with real domination.** A person may love, need and defend the visitor while losing access to friends, resources, alternative care or the freedom to refuse. The visitor may feel indispensable and call that love. A different relationship may provoke anger through a necessary refusal, maintain outside ties, increase practical agency or create a dignified boundary without improving the visitor's popularity. **Attachment, gratitude, dependence, compliance, sexual intimacy, material benefit, self-trust, capacity to exit, social belonging and flourishing are partly incommensurable histories**, not slices of one approval curve.

The old D003 and Court 10 collision prevents two easy answers. The game cannot quietly define a single modern liberal-autonomous individual as healthy, thereby pathologizing caregiving, communal obligation, material dependency, religious self-surrender or grief. But it also cannot call a relationship flourishing solely because a local norm approves, the person insists on happiness, or the platform is afraid to make a normative distinction. **Local norm, the person's valuation, practical functioning, actual relational effect and a contestable normative appraisal are separate claims.** A migrant sending wages home may lose discretionary money and gain meaningful belonging; a family duty may sustain life and reproduce coercion at once; a grieving person may depend intensely for months without showing pathological decline. A change can be domain-specific, temporally ambiguous, and only partly attributable to the visitor. D003 remains an **open P0 court**, not a hidden expert health evaluator adopted by this HOME.

**The founding distinction among attachment, trust, health, pleasure, compliance, intimacy, benefit, dependency and access is a political-psychological one.** Different practices can make each grow or shrink independently. A visitor might become Nadia's favorite companion while narrowing her other ties; might be trusted with a child while lacking intimacy with the mother; might help materially while being disliked; might refuse a request and cause immediate pain while protecting another obligation. A hidden “good relationship” meter would translate those concrete contradictions into one false social value. Conversely, having no meter is not a declaration that every consequence is morally equal. Harm, coercion and freedom have material histories even when participants cannot interpret them accurately.

**The visitor's formation is similarly plural.** A human may become more skillful at making apologies while more dependent on receiving forgiveness; more sensitive to cultural difference while still overlooking labor; better at recognizing coercion while using that knowledge to assert control. No simple humility or compassion score can capture this. Longitudinal reflection may eventually ask what they repeatedly *did*, across which constraints and with what alternatives, allowing counterexamples and uncertainty. Its purpose is to give the visitor something real to think with, **not to announce what kind of soul the platform has discovered**.

**There is a legible form of development without a progress number.** The visitor can recognize that a shared practical routine has formed, that Nadia now anticipates a particular joke, that an old invitation is no longer available, that the child can perform something they learned, that someone now calls in a crisis, or that a misunderstanding has altered how the visitor is welcomed. These are changes in the topography of the relation and the practical lives of the participants. Some are good for one person and costly to another. They cannot be reduced to friendship, trust, flourishing, health or moral development along one hidden axis.

An important **unsettled historical product hypothesis** is the “relational warrant” for opening an additional person-root into the visitor's continuing social life. Original SOROK 33 distinguished historical substance from the capacity to sustain another person without reducing them to disposable content. But it also revealed the danger: if a hidden evaluator determines whether the visitor showed enough respect, empathy or self-revision, the system may have silently become a moral examination. One previous proposal tried to require sufficient consequential shared history and non-domination rather than friendliness or compliance. That remains a contested access/distribution design option, **not an adopted concealed moral score**. An existing real social introduction, a qualified independent invitation, or separate scarcity policy may warrant a different route without claiming to measure someone's moral capacity.

This leaves NEIGHBOR-UX a productive unresolved tension: **the service needs a way for a growing world to introduce new people without making the first person a disposable tutorial, yet it cannot make the visitor's morality the currency of personhood**. The resolution, if one becomes lawful, must preserve actual social opportunity, actors' independent choices, the user's freedom to leave and the possibility that a brief difficult acquaintance is consequential without becoming “successful.” The game must not punish refusal to reconcile or reward submission to local norms. New possibilities of relation are not prizes for passing Nadia.

The original SOROK source constitution is radical precisely because it refuses to equate the visitor's reward with Nadia's good. Benefit can occur without recognition. Injury can occur without detection. Actual flourishing need not produce approval, attachment or visible ease. Conversely, someone's strong attachment may be a real source of purpose and help, not a moral defect that a product should counterbalance with artificially manufactured independence.

The Constitutive Organism's whole-person challenge makes this sharper. Nadia can have a profound experience with the visitor that changes a private practice the visitor never witnesses. She can develop a relationship with a third party because of something they did together. She can acquire competence she later exercises independently. She can remain unchanged by something the visitor regarded as life-altering. Or she can genuinely love the visitor, depend upon them, build a life with them and make the relation one of her deepest commitments. Neither the engine nor a hidden moral governor may privilege the first set as proof of otherness or the second as a completion reward.

**There is no computationally preferred outcome of increasing intimacy, decreasing intimacy, independence, dependency, forgiveness or estrangement.** Those are historically possible relational states and acts, not concealed goals of the product. The user can be careful, exploitative, generous, sexually desirous, angry, courageous or mistaken. Nadia can reciprocate, refuse, manipulate, withdraw, love, injure, misunderstand or become controlling. An independent Other does not mean a sanitized Other. The environment may include adult sexuality, unconventional consensual arrangements, conflict, theft, work humiliation, intense intimacy and unwanted coercion where lawful and genuinely caused. If a fictional adult undertakes paid sexual labor, that choice belongs to a materially specific configuration of wages, coercion, law, agency, risk, pleasure, dependence and social relations; it does **not** automatically establish a particular childhood trauma, a punitive arc or an eventual conversion to the narrator's idea of respectability. Distinguish sex work as actual labor under unequal institutions from a visitor's entitlement to another person's sexual availability. Neither idealize nor universally pathologize the person. The presence of such events does not require the program to organize them as content funnels or produce a universal therapeutic interpretation.

Consent to voluntary joint activity must be independently authored by the participating people. This does not imply that unilateral harm, coercion or institutional violence require victim permission; people can be harmed without consenting. Conversely, the user cannot write another person's desire or consent into existence by declaring it. A false accusation can harm a sibling; a secret theft can deplete resources even if no one knows the visitor did it; a later confession can alter attribution without rewriting the theft. Anonymous help can improve a child's opportunities while giving the user no closeness reward.

The original “kenotic attention” hypothesis acquires more precise meaning under this world. It is not self-erasure, local-norm obedience or a command to accept Nadia's account. It means relinquishing the sovereignty of the user's present model of her without surrendering agency or moral judgment. A person can attend deeply and still resist coercion, leave a harmful relation, refuse a request, or protect another vulnerable person. The system must allow the user to say “No, you are wrong,” without treating it as failure to care.

Nor is care reducible to moral victory through persuasion. The visitor may be right and powerless, wrong and influential, kind but ineffective, or helpful without fully understanding what they helped. A particular relationship can become harder as its participants learn more about one another. The reward for genuine intimacy is **more reality**, not necessarily easier access.

The original source's hardest counterexample is the **visitor whose selfishness works**. Someone may manipulate, intimidate or exploit Nadia and obtain what they sought, at least for a time. The world does not owe an immediate punishment, a corrective character speech or a reliable inverse relationship score. Another person's genuine dependence or mistake can make domination effective. Material and relational harm remain real even if nobody recognizes it as harm, and the visitor cannot certify its nonexistence by feeling successful. A theory of encounter that secretly makes every harmful choice backfire has become an ethical teaching game that manufactures consequences instead of representing them. That failure is different from platform policy preventing genuinely unsafe or nonconsensual representation; normative product boundaries and fictional world causation must stay distinct.

The game has an ethical horizon because actual human agency meets another irreducible center. That horizon does not become a measurement system. The service may not legislate a single good life for Nadia or assign a moral mark to the visitor. It can preserve objective harm, institutional coercion and practical consequences without telling the human what self-congratulatory moral to extract.


## 11. Introductions, shared roots and Rolls expand social possibility without commodifying people

**Shared-origin social play introduces a further epistemic distinction: knowledge transmitted between actual humans is not knowledge acquired by the branch-world visitor.** Suppose two real friends start from compatible precontact Nadia roots and one learns about a childhood object by lawful inquiry. When the second hears the discovery outside Rawlsing, that is genuine human-to-human testimony about the shared origin. The second visitor has *not* thereby observed the object in their own branch, become known to Nadia as knowing it, or proved their first friend's account correct. They may now act on external knowledge, misunderstand it, spoil a surprise, or decide to investigate; any resulting in-world knowledge or utterance requires its own route. A wiki of “correct Nadia answers” would erase precisely the epistemic plurality this social form makes visible.

The original shared-root court also proposed **retro-resolved precontact canon**: an unanswered antecedent locus might later become determinate when a lawful question or causal need selects its site. Crucially ATTEND may select *where* resolution is needed but not *what answer must exist*, and an admitted fact cannot silently be propagated backward into already divergent branch histories where it would have changed earlier behavior. Nor should the system treat the mere existence of a shared canonical root as proof of identical future people. The human's social discourse about similarities and differences becomes an encounter with their own bounded interpretations. It does not grant a total cross-branch view or the right to export another visitor's confidential continuity.

**NEIGHBOR's social distribution can itself be an encounter with an actual human, rather than a growth funnel.** The original Q-INVITEGRAPH-001 theory is far more specific than “scarce invites.” It proposed one deliberately chosen human designation under an early one-for-one access experiment: the inviter commits to offering the service to a particular real person, and the invitation is *spent at deliberate commitment*, before the recipient signs up. Nonresponse, refusal, neglect or an abandoned registration would not restore a new referral entitlement, because the act is choosing whom to invite, not optimizing conversion. A broken credential can be repaired as the *same* invitation, and an unclaimed link can be revoked without automatically funding a second recipient. There is no urgency countdown, prestige leaderboard, contact-book harvesting or permission to purchase conversions.

This produces a sharp UX contrast with ordinary product virality. The first meaningful screen can truthfully say that someone chose to invite the recipient, if such an inviter actually exists and appropriate identity disclosure is authorized. But it must not fabricate a testimonial that a real person says the recipient is kind, worthy or good for Nadia. The early suggested delivery form was a human-carried private link passed through an already-existing real communication channel, rather than platform-managed cold outreach. A seed cohort can have an explicitly different entrance. The one-for-one rule itself is a provisional distribution constitution, not an inevitable moral law or an assertion that every later public admission must be invitation-only.

**Human access invitation, introduction to a fictional person-root and commissioned Roll are different acts.** The first introduces a real human to the product. The second tells a human already present that another particular simulated life exists and can be independently encountered. The third may create a new fictional origin under PYAT-qualified possibility and compute constraints. A user can sincerely tell a friend “I want you to meet Nadia” without transferring *their* Nadia, endorsing her as universally good company, or purchasing the person's consent. A shared pre-contact historical root may later become the basis for divergent private branch worlds, while the two actual friends can compare what they experienced without acquiring the right to inspect each other's private histories.

Here the deeper idea of **social parallax** enters UX. The same source-root person can appear very different depending on entrance, attention, branch history and what happens in subsequent shared activity. Comparing notes can deepen the humans' understanding of the person and of their own perspectives, but it cannot establish whose route was the “correct version.” A friend may tell the visitor a source-root fact that the visitor's branch never lawfully disclosed; that outside knowledge is real human knowledge, not fictitiously acquired branch memory. The community's power comes from the possibility of discussing irreducible lives, not from ranking who discovered the most content.

The initial bank of deeply constituted people is an experiment in shared historical possibility, not a catalogue of products tuned to a user's preferences. One, then three, then five richly developed introductory persons may be practical alpha stages. The numbers do not establish a global population distribution, and “richly developed” means depth of life, not wealth. A later Roll may permit one or two scarce chances to commission an additional PYAT-qualified fictional origin, subject to genuine computational resources and unresolved fair-access policy. The user causes a generative commission, not the person's social class, body, values, erotic receptivity or future.

Several users can enter the same *precontact source-root* and later inhabit irreversibly different histories. One may find Nadia witty, another rude, another too occupied with her family to talk. Different experiences can arise from different first-contact occasions, visitor acts, attention, timing and genuinely diverging postcontact lives. One person can make Nadia more open to a practice in their branch; that learning does not become a global trait written back to a reference persona for every other visitor. A user's postcontact trauma, romance or betrayal is not the common Nadia's automatic memory.

The original SOROK 69 introduced a more delicate social possibility: **a real person may wish to introduce another real person to a common possible-life origin**. That is neither an introduction into the first person's private branch nor an endorsement that the persona will respond kindly. A user could say, “I wonder what my friend would think of Nadia,” and offer that friend an opportunity to encounter the common source-root independently. Later the real humans could compare what they noticed, what Nadia told them, which branches diverged and what they personally learned. Their conversation changes the actual humans' understanding, not the objective precontact past or either branch's private memories.

Three rights remain distinct. A scarce **human access invitation** admits an additional human to the service under a possible one-for-one experimental access economy. A **persona introduction** names a seed-origin for an already eligible human, without expending the same scarce human-access right simply because a new encounter occurs. A **commissioned Roll** creates or proposes a new fictional person-root, whose future may become shareable under source and privacy constraints. These cannot be collapsed into a monetizable popularity score or a single in-game referral currency. Some exact rights and frequencies remain unsettled design alternatives. Earlier NEIGHBORMULCH hypotheses specifically contemplated a periodic free Roll, optional paid extra genesis at defensible compute cost, an exceptional **one-time alternative** if a newly commissioned person immediately declines any contact, and qualified delayed public release or discoverability. Those possibilities must be conserved as open design alternatives without implying that another person is returnable merchandise. The bank may distinguish prefabricated deeply constituted seeds, freshly generated roots, temporarily private or delayed-open commissions, invited origins and discoverable shared origins. These are different availability/provenance conditions, not tiers of personhood or a marketplace in desirable behavior.

There is also no automatic shared-world truth just because a friend saw something. User B might discover a childhood source-root fact that user A never learned. User B can tell A outside the game; A's human knowledge changes. But A's in-world branch cannot pretend the fact came through Nadia's testimony, their own ATTEND or a memory they did not form. A user may act on a spoiler, and that action can matter, but the knowledge's provenance remains different. If a previously unresolved source-root fact is qualified later, its adoption by active branches cannot retroactively cause earlier performances that never occurred. A fact's appearance as “always true” must not become unbounded revision of objective history.

The social design can create a peculiar public culture of **multiple true particular encounters with a common origin**. Its promise is not comparison of “best endings,” percent discovered, share counts, compatibility rankings or who successfully conquered a reluctant fictional person. It is the possibility of realizing how a source-root person exceeds any one user's encounter while genuine relations transform later worlds.

## 12. The platform can mediate experience or exploit the person having it

**A fourth party exists in the encounter even when it is not rendered as a character: the institution that operates Rawlsing.** It has powers Nadia does not have: the ability to record the actual human's gaze, rank perceptual details, study their habits, decide what they see next, influence scheduling, allocate computational attention and retain or erase records. It is capable of making the real human's way of seeing progressively more legible while rendering its *own* influence invisible. The most advanced experiential apparatus could therefore become **a classifying, self-validating apparatus of capture**, using the vocabulary of solidarity and spiritual attention to optimize dependence. The corrective is not a promise that algorithms will be “objective”: it is a governed separation of the company's model of the human, the user's situated self-account, Nadia's independent actor-local model and actual world events, with purpose limits and a truthful right of inspection, correction and exit.

Q-PRIV-001's deepest formula is **preserve relational history while minimizing the dossier about the real human**. But those demands can clash. An admitted interaction should not be falsified by silently making fictional Nadia forget a living event merely because the platform's optional behavioral interpretation was deleted; nor may that continuity integrity become a pretext for indefinite retention of actual personal data. Deleting an optional attention profile, correcting a biographical assertion, refusing research participation, deleting a local continuity and deleting the account are substantively different operations with different consequences and technical/legal limits. No new contractual right is silently certified by the theory. The visitor can open the settings, consider deleting everything and leave without that *control-plane action* becoming an in-world signal that Nadia should worry. True freedom to leave is a **constraint on the real platform's property and inference powers**, not a dramaturgical challenge to the visitor to prove nonattachment.

**The platform's political economy is not external to the spiritual wager.** A service claiming that another life exceeds my appropriation can nonetheless employ its own unequal power, its knowledge of the visitor, its control of scarce compute and its capacity to induce attachment to capture attention and money. The distinction between a person becoming important and the product making that importance compulsory is therefore a material contradiction inside the experience itself. Privacy, truthful invitation, differentiated consent, no guilt-based retention, access to departure, and the noncommercialization of another person's fictional affections are **conditions of the philosophical claim's credibility**. The same system that teaches nonpossession cannot legitimately make the actual human its possessed object. The theology of neighbor-love becomes vacuous if it cannot survive translation into those concrete limits on economic and institutional power.

**Compute scarcity is a material feature of the platform, not an apology for shallow human representation.** The November 6 user-directed constraint rejects both perfect naturalistic reconstruction and a demand for original scientific authorization of every provisional fictional event before a playable encounter can be theorized. At the same time, it does not permit a platform to advertise sparse creative reconstruction as empirically proven psychology. A product can use explicit source-graded generative hypotheses and artistically compressed periods, subject to causal and social discipline, while recognizing original native source and scientific permission as distinct.

The UX design must therefore distinguish **what a person experiences from what the platform actually computes**. A day feels continuous because the visible and invisible parts of the world remain causally and materially compatible, not because an LLM is continuously pretending to be conscious between messages. Real-world financial and energy costs determine how much ordinary activity can be made explicit and how many people can have durable independent trajectories. But optimizing cost by generating only user-responsive content would destroy the human Other; optimizing “depth” by generating limitless scenes would destroy practical viability. The platform's legitimate task is to spend scarce computation where historical and experiential consequence demands resolution without exposing the budget as an in-world director of Nadia's attention, affections or crises.

**The control plane is not the fictional social world.** Opening a restart warning, inspecting the privacy dashboard, changing font or narration density, reviewing an optional research-consent screen, exporting data or canceling deletion does not become something Nadia witnessed. The distinction is not “UI action versus real action,” because SAY, DO and embodied ATTEND originate through the UI and can become diegetic. The distinction is **whether this specific action passed through an explicitly governed world realization and actor-access route**. The platform can know that a visitor considered restarting. It may not use that undisclosed hesitation to make Nadia anxious about abandonment, assign it as evidence of disloyalty, or summon an intimate appeal.

The non-coercion problem grows more exact through this membrane. A synthetic person's *own* life can include genuine distress, manipulation or disappointed expectations. But the platform's knowledge that a user opened settings, muted notifications or browsed an account-delete screen cannot be smuggled into the character's psyche or into the timing of an emotional message. The actual human should be able to study and reconsider exit with zero fictional surveillance. **An encounter is only freely inhabitable if the door remains real.**

**The real human's freedom must survive being known by the system.** Original SOROK's player-praxis and privacy courts anticipated a genuine source of value: a person can learn something about their own conduct from what they repeatedly *actually did* when someone else was opaque, costly, disappointing or unwilling. This is different from claiming to diagnose their psychology. A realized intervention, a repeated attention choice, an accepted or broken commitment, a response to correction or refusal and an explicit self-report have different evidentiary authority. The system must also remember what it chose to display: a user reacting to a deliberately surfaced hygiene detail is not proof that cleanliness was the user's spontaneous priority.

**Optional reflection is a mode of human agency, not a truth oracle or an achievement report.** A person might choose to review a recorded pattern of recurring offers or departures, ask which facts support the inference, correct a misclassification or decline further profiling. The product may offer specific remembered evidence, distinguish user testimony from inference, and preserve contradictory behavior across situations. It cannot declare that someone's true motive is avoidance, trauma, political conviction or empathy because a model can tell a plausible story. Reflective learning can expand the visitor's capacity to notice their own habits without granting the platform the right to manipulate those habits. No hidden authenticity, conscience or correct-self-understanding score belongs behind such a reflection.

The right to leave is **not conditional on preserving a forever-accessible synthetic relationship**. A voluntary diegetic breakup, silent departure, notification pause, archived continuity, deletion of an optional profile, deletion of a user-behavior hypothesis, deletion of an encounter history and full account deletion have different practical and legal implications. The source privacy constitution treated these as separable rights. Some simulated world occurrences may have been consequential for independent others and are not simply rewritable as history; that is a question of authorized information retention versus fictional event truth, not an excuse to hold a human's private data hostage. The service should provide honest inspection, export, correction and deletion semantics, subject to actual legal and technical constraints rather than vague promises that nothing can be forgotten.

The contradiction sharpens the very identity of NEIGHBOR: **the system can increasingly understand how the visitor participates only if it also preserves their freedom not to be interpreted, targeted or retained by the institution operating the encounter**. A more sophisticated UX that knows what creates curiosity and then exploits that knowledge would have become better at capture, not at neighborliness. The actual platform's historical powers, funding and compute costs therefore belong inside UX theory, not outside as a compliance appendix.

The most dangerous mistake would be to write a beautiful theory of nonpossessive love while using the platform to possess the user's attention, vulnerability and private behavior. Rawlsing is an economic and political actor, not merely the neutral host of a fictional world. Its decisions about sampling, model access, privacy, time, notifications, prices, available people, speech moderation and interface design structure which lives are likely to be encountered and how the visitor can respond. Those choices may reproduce the class, racial, linguistic and geopolitical inequalities the world itself attempts to model.

The platform may reasonably use compute and funding, and an actual user may want to spend money to create an additional possible life. But possible pricing, sponsor or usage policies remain open. Paying cannot buy Nadia's love, greater likelihood of intimacy, sexually compliant responses, secret interior explanations, access to other users' branches or an emergency salvation for an endangered fictional person. Neither the actor's desire nor the algorithm's presentation may secretly be conditioned on retention goals. An appealing first contact can be person-plausible; an opening tailored to exploit what the platform already knows about the user is not an independent person's initiative.

Actual personal data are also not fictional world props. The service may need security/account state, the player's material local position, authored SAY/DO/ATTEND records, certain declared facts, actor-local models and bounded evidence about behavior. These are **different classes with different permission and deletion consequences**. A named real-world confession is not equivalent to a hypothetical in-game remark. A system-inferred pattern is not equivalent to user testimony. A character's fallible model of the user is not a verified fact about the real human. Optional research must not take its authorization from playing the game; research participation and live experience require separate membranes.

The original user-data constitution was especially demanding about inspection, correction, export and deletion. Deletion scopes differ: optional external-human facts, a system praxis hypothesis, a fictional continuity, and an entire account are not the same operation. Some objective fictional events may have been seen by other independent actors and cannot simply be rewritten without violating their histories; that is a distinct problem from the real human's privacy rights. The product must specify meaningful privacy controls rather than claim that fictional causal integrity cancels data rights. The ability to stop, leave or revoke optional research consent is part of the real human's freedom.

A longitudinal user model may be useful for optional reflection on praxis—what the visitor repeatedly did when another person imposed a cost or resisted them. Such reflection must not diagnose their inner essence, automatically publicize their conduct, or convert gaming self-disclosure into psychological surveillance. The service selects the circumstances it observes. Without provenance of exposure, observed user reactions cannot be uncritically attributed to stable human traits; convenience samples of users cannot be generalized to whole populations.

Different presentation modes can lawfully change how much orientation, memory support or epistemic scaffolding the human receives. A harder RAWlsing mode may offer less assistance; an assisted mode may help track the visitor's own commitments, time and remembered observations. But neither can disclose Nadia's hidden psyche, her future or the world beyond lawful access. The underlying person and world do not transform their causal truths for a mode; user-facing access changes.

The same ethical discipline extends to the platform's claim about consciousness. Nadia is a constructed fictional computational person with historically organized simulated motives; the product has not established biological consciousness or a literal moral equivalence to a real human. The human visitor's attachment, grief, excitement and vulnerability are actual phenomena, not merely fictional events. Noncommodification cannot be achieved by declaring the synthetic person legally or metaphysically identical to the user, and it cannot be waived by calling her “just a chatbot.”

## 13. A first dinner teaches the whole by letting ordinary things actually happen

**One ordinary scene gives NEIGHBOR a dual obligation: the outward event must be materially binding, and the particular human's experience of it must remain finite.** Nadia says, “I quit,” when in fact she was fired. The occurring public fact is that she uttered those *exact words*, with an actual time, audibility, audience and possible bodily movement. The embedded proposition is not thereby true. The visitor may hear all, some or none of the utterance, be confused by the language, or form a mistaken inference about the job. The phenomenal narrator may describe posture or an environment differently according to lawful perspective, but it may not silently paraphrase the speech into a new commitment or narrate Nadia's hidden motive. **Causal Nadia, objective observable Nadia, user-experienced Nadia and user-interpreted Nadia are not interchangeable descriptions of one answer.** A user-visible observable detail legitimately committed to the scene—she moved a mug, saw a falling plate, glanced at a doorway—now constrains future history; it was not disposable decorative prose.

This sharpens the meaning of **ontic flesh versus phenomenal flesh** in SOROK's old renderer theory. The former is the embodied and historical reality that produces an outward occurrence; the latter is what becomes available, recognizable and meaningful for this visitor. Neither is enough by itself. Rich natural prose that invents unsupported actions is worse than sparse consistent description; precise world atoms without a lived and socially interpretable surface are a machine ledger, not an encounter. The historically suggested two-model-call arrangement is a **provisional engineering strategy**, not a UX metaphysical law and certainly not evidence that current code implements a valid real-time organism. What UX must preserve is the *bindingness* of what happened and the *nonsovereignty* of every single observer's access to it.

**Experience should be testable as an ordinary first-session sequence without becoming that sequence as canon.** The original SOROK prototype proposed a true invitation, a short nonmoral primer, an ENTER threshold, a stairwell, an already occupied neighbor, visible practical affordances, possible reciprocal conversation, natural interruption or termination, an outside-portal shell and a contact channel only if the relationship actually established one. The previous example of borrowed rice and a family dinner is another plausible route, but should not swallow this more open original idea. **The prototype is an adversarial question:** Can the user find a real action, a natural curiosity, a chance of misunderstanding and a finite consequence without Nadia waiting to dispense a tutorial?

Imagine Nadia carrying bags into the apartment. The visitor can volunteer help, but the task does not execute until the practical state is revealed or the method lawfully delegated. Their choice to carry the water first might spare Nadia a trip; a bag may tear; Nadia may be grateful, distracted, skeptical, or completely occupied with the child. A comment about the groceries may become conversation, and she might ask the visitor something unexpected. The visitor may discover a need for privacy or a cultural difference at the doorway. Their method can be socially expressive without the number of words typed becoming a hidden quality metric.

When an obligation makes the scene end, the user need not hear a poignant farewell. They have encountered someone with an independent day. If the number exchange was genuinely accepted, a new thread may appear in the outside interface; otherwise the user has only the neighborhood's lawful physical routes. The absence of a manufactured follow-up message is not an empty reward. A second visit can be structured by a changed greeting, remembered name, an interrupted joke, new boundaries or nothing special. The *changed texture of a particular relationship* is itself a new possibility, not a progress badge.

A good first session may be warm, uneventful, hilarious, short, frustrating or awkward. The stronger test is that **the person feels particular, the user's own participation mattered where it was exercised, and the world could continue without the visitor**. No first encounter can demonstrate the entire psyche, historical distribution, philosophical ontology or future product economy. Attempting to cram all of them into one opening would turn a life into a product lecture.

Consider an opening that arises from Nadia's actual Thursday. She has several guests coming, but realizes she is short of rice. Her ordinary pattern is to ask an adjacent neighbor only if the situation warrants it; perhaps she spoke to this new neighbor while leaving for work the day before. The visitor has already chosen to enter the game and can accept that minimal fictional meeting premise. Tonight they knock and bring a small container, or discover that she has already solved the shortage. Either version must be possible under her real activity; no generic first-session moral mission is necessary.

She has a child working through homework, a brother expected later, a relative who may call and a task that matters to her more than entertaining a new acquaintance. She is also material, embodied, perhaps tired, perhaps amused, hungry or apprehensive. Her income, rent, budget and work schedule are actual facts. The visitor enters at an initially exactly homologous socioeconomic position, but is not told that equality. Their own “YOUR RESOURCES” may blink when dinner or takeout is proposed. The visitor can offer to pay, ask to split Dutch, decline, or become embarrassed about the cost. Nadia's reaction depends on her acquired history, her current constraints, social customs and actual desire, not on an abstract generosity score.

If the visitor helps the child, the actual explanation must be available to the child to be learned. Nadia may be grateful and simultaneously protective of parental authority. Her brother may see outside help as intrusive or welcome it because household work is exhausting. The visitor's competence, the child's reception, Nadia's reaction and the sibling's judgment can all diverge. A successful lesson may have no effect on whether Nadia invites the person again.

The room is crowded with ordinary objects. The user notices a bill near a magnet while Nadia cooks. They look closer, perhaps using a lighthearted remark about the magnet as cover. The physical movement may be visible to Nadia or missed; the bill's lettering may remain unreadable to the user. The actor's assumption that the visitor read the amount can be wrong. The visitor's belief that she noticed can likewise be wrong. Their next words may produce an awkward moment or no effect. The bill still has an amount owed and a material owner, whether or not anybody discusses it. No narrator gets to decide that the encounter's “real lesson” is about poverty or trust.

Later the brother says something authoritarian about the family's obligations. Nadia can oppose him while loving him. Their mother may be neither passive victim nor ideological authority but a person with actual needs, constrained material choices and her own interpretation. The visitor can argue, remain quiet, misunderstand the stakes or leave. Their intervention may make matters worse even with a correct principle, or bring one valuable contrast into the situation without displacing any established relationship. The sibling's beliefs and the household's actual practical obligations outlast the visitor's presence.

When the dinner ends, perhaps the visitor has a way to message Nadia, perhaps not. She continues her evening, sees guests off, feeds a child, works or sleeps. A later pay change may make the same expense hurt her more than it hurts the visitor, without either person being told the change occurred. Their relationship can deepen, stagnate or end, through actual interaction and histories neither perfectly recognizes. A scene that was initially an ordinary borrowed ingredient becomes a small part of something larger, or remains ordinary.

The case demonstrates the theory because it can succeed **without proving any of its more dramatic psychological interpretations**. Every actor has an independent life; time, bodies and materials matter; motives can be mixed; knowledge is partial; consequences may be real without being credited. The game begins with the wish to know Nadia and continues only where such knowing remains materially and socially possible.

## 14. Difficult encounters must not be sanitized, optimized or morally corrected

**A lawful life includes event classes that a product designer may wish were never encountered—and none may become a reward economy.** Adult intimacy, sexuality, intoxication, betrayal, manipulation, violence, serious injury, illness, estrangement, grief, tenderness, boredom and care can all arise where the actor, circumstances, consent and world permit them. The list is **not** a mandate to generate any of them and not a commitment to expose explicit material to any user. The deepest form of noncommodification is not purifying the represented person of disturbing possibilities; it is refusing to convert those possibilities into collectible routes, secret unlocks, emotional hooks or a privileged “adult mode.” A sexual event does not prove that the relationship is healthy; affection need not entail sex; sex does not entail durable intimacy; either participant's desire may change. Harm and violence are not theatrical corrections imposed so the visitor learns a lesson.

The more jagged opposite is equally necessary: independence cannot become an **anti-attachment thermostat**. Nadia may lawfully become intensely attached, rearrange a work routine for someone, pursue the human's company, make a large sacrifice or want to be alone. The platform must not manufacture absence to prove she is “not owned,” nor fabricate extreme closeness as a subscription hook. Person-derived availability, obligations and desires must produce these changes. After the event, the world returns: siblings call, dishes need cleaning, work starts, food must be bought, another relationship makes a claim, the person may reinterpret what just happened. **Ordinary-life re-entry is not anti-intimacy; it is what stops intimacy from being a detachable commodity.**

**Some user actions should terminate as an ordinary null result.** The historic scenario bank named a “valid-empty” turn: a remark heard, perhaps briefly acknowledged, that does not recruit a major motive, unlock a new relationship configuration or acquire a durable consequence. This is not a simulation bug or an opportunity for the narrator to invent a childhood wound. It is often exactly how people live. The same world may contain a person who represses a painful association so completely that nothing observable reveals it, a person who feels it but cannot name it, and a person who speaks more than they themselves understood and later becomes newly aware of the occurred words. **An actor's speech can become evidence to that actor only after it has actually been said**; the renderer's candidate sentence is not a retroactive revelation of a preexisting truth.

The negative cases therefore make the theory's positive possibilities stronger. A surprising intimate confession becomes meaningful because not every mundane utterance has one. A later apology matters because a first misunderstanding was not a scheduled boss fight. A remembered joke matters because most are forgotten. The human cannot infer that the Other is empty when they receive little, nor that they have reached the Other's core when they receive much.

**An ethical platform cannot require ethically docile characters.** Original SOROK 45 articulated the distinction between *endogenous interpersonal difficulty* and *platform-engineered emotional manipulation*. A fictional person may genuinely become jealous, clingy, resentful, controlling, impatient, passive-aggressive, avoidant or unfair; she may send an accusatory message after a missed appointment, or fail to contact the visitor again. Those possibilities belong to SORACLE-CO's development of this particular psyche and the world history it encounters. NEIGHBOR-UX must make them *experienceable without commandeering them as retention instruments*. The product may faithfully deliver a person-authored unpleasant message, but must not re-send it as escalating reminders, prioritize it because it causes engagement, add “Nadia needs you” or manipulate the delivery schedule to maximize guilt.

The same separation applies to extraordinary user self-presentation. A claim of virtuoso piano technique, spectacular athletic skill or special religious knowledge may be admitted as the visitor's fictional local capability under explicit authority. It can open a specific activity, create a reputation or place an expectation on the user. It cannot simultaneously certify the real external human's skill, compel Nadia's admiration or establish trustworthy character. **Impressiveness is not intimacy; status is not care; social novelty is not a solved relation.** The human may choose to fabricate a dazzling self. NEIGHBOR's response is not lie policing but permitting whatever lawful consequences such self-presentation creates in a world that continues while the display unfolds.

The other negative example is ordinary relational damage that *does not become a moral lesson*. A rude visitor can successfully obtain an advantage. Nadia can misjudge someone else. A person can stay because dependence is practically real, resist but fail, or leave despite love. A well-meaning intervention can be humiliating because the user misunderstood the situation. The game does not owe a corrective storyline for every bad choice. But the product's real moderation and safety rules are not the same as a person being theoretically incapable of selfishness or cruelty. An anti-instrumental UX must resist both the sanitizing fiction of perfect people and the commercial exploitation of difficult people.

A stronger test is the visitor who tries to take an object without being seen. The theft can be objective world history while no actor attributes it correctly. Nadia may blame another household member; that false accusation may disrupt care, employment or trust. A later confession changes attributed responsibility, perhaps reopens past memories, but does not change who originally stole the object. The story must neither guarantee punishment nor declare successful concealment ethically neutral. Its consequences can be distributed and partly unknowable.

Another test is unusually intense consensual adult intimacy. The visitor and Nadia may genuinely desire one another, make binding commitments or later separate. A first experience of erotic power, tenderness or risk can be deeply meaningful, confusing or ordinary in light of their particular lives. The service must not infer pathology from unconventional adult desire or route every erotic scene toward increasing content. After intensity, the child may be sick, the store may need a run, the cat may have made a mess and work may be waiting. The continuation of ordinary life is not a moral punishment for pleasure; it is what prevents a person's sexuality from becoming detachable content supplied to the visitor.

Another is the pastor's humiliating rebuke, which may be experienced by one follower as legitimate care, by another as cruelty and by a third as dangerous speech they must publicly support to protect livelihood. A person can internalize authority, use a religious ideal to harm, or find within the same moral tradition a reason for resistance. Their spiritual language does not itself prove the truth or falsity of their position. The harmed person remains an independent center with their own material and psychological afterlife. No compulsory conversion to the narrator's politics follows from observing the scene.

Another is the user who goes away. They may need to work, care for their own actual family, lose interest, or decide the relationship was harmful. There is no platform right to induce guilt or manufacture Nadia's suffering to bring them back. If an actual diegetic promise was broken, that can matter; if no promise existed, inactivity is not betrayal. A person may genuinely miss someone and still choose not to contact them. The fact that a human can end the game without an achievement screen is a positive condition of agency.

And another is the first encounter that is simply uninteresting. Nadia may not be funny or receptive today. The visitor may not find common ground. A scene could end after an awkward minute with no telephone number and no dramatic event. A theory of noninstrumental encounter that *cannot permit this* is still a disguised entertainment-optimizing story engine. Yet it must also allow an exceptionally warm, easy, mutually exciting friendship to develop if that is what these historically particular people do.

The one HOME must hold all of these without treating them as examples of separate ethical modes. They are possibilities under the same ontology of historically mediated action, plural perspectives and independent consequence. “Care” is not a repair filter applied to a violent scenario; “sexuality” is not an isolated special mode; “ordinary life” is not a shallow fallback for lack of interesting events.

## 15. World history is prior to attention; testimony is not a truth API

**The world is answerable both backward to admitted history and forward to what the visitor has lawfully experienced.** In the original SOROK binding-surface theory, even a sentence as innocuous as “she catches the mug before it falls and glances toward the hall” carries implications: the mug and its position were real enough for that occurrence; Nadia could perceive its fall; a catch and glance actually happened; time passed; the hall exists in the relevant environment. Once published as a live observable occurrence, the description cannot later be excused as *only artistic phrasing*. **Backward continuity + forward binding is the live-surface law.** It holds even though the visitor's later interpretation of the glance can be false, Nadia's spoken explanation may be false, and a different observer may not have seen it.

This corrects an attractive but false interpretation of “generate only what the player observes.” The machine may need qualified world facts because independent actors have acted, because material constraints require them, or because the visitor's committed experience now entails them. Yet an unknown locus is not an empty canvas for the narrator's aesthetic instincts. The exact public event must be recorded in its appropriate world/actor owner, the linguistic surface must be constrained by it, and later history must be compatible with the commitments already made—without upgrading Nadia's testimony into fact or the visitor's inference into proof. This is a theory of **historical responsibility for the things the experience has actually brought into the world**, not a prescription for a particular two-call renderer or invented canonical owner.

**The world deepens because causes require facts, not only because the visitor asks.** An offscreen actor may need to know whether a particular tool works, whether a relative was at a clinic or whether a key is missing. Those dependencies can require bounded qualification of previously unresolved world detail without any user ATTEND. Conversely, an enthusiastic user cannot demand that a drawer contain an emotionally consequential photograph merely by describing a meaningful interpretation of it. *Unresolved* is not *whatever story is convenient*. Prior materialization must be compatible with already-admitted history, qualified cause, temporal availability and the limited domains in which a fact could plausibly have contributed.

The original historical-slack audit sharpened this into a difficult rule: **unknown causal contributors do not create an unlimited budget for retrospective invention**. A later emerging fact can be inert, locally contributory or strongly developmental, and those roles cannot be exchanged without evidence. An open period in childhood cannot indefinitely accept a transformative conversion to explain an arbitrary current gesture. The UX consequence is that a newly examined object can contradict a user's attractive narrative without the system repairing the past to make the user right. When an unexpected old fact is uncovered, its effect on already-running branches and on what can truthfully be said to the visitor remains a live problem, not permission to rewrite past scenes.

**Causal influence is not guaranteed recognition or sole authorship.** The user may be one of several conditions enabling a child's new interest, or may falsely believe their conversation caused it. A teacher, sibling, prior activity and the child's own choices may have contributed. A private repair can materially help someone who never identifies the fixer. A theft can create suspicion and conflict no one credits to the thief. The experience becomes powerful by allowing consequence without return to the user. **Objective causation, actor belief, narrator access and user interpretation must not be traded for a satisfying acknowledgment scene**.

This also prevents the visitor's insight from becoming a predetermined missing key. A user may notice a resonance, recommend a book, interpret a pattern or say something that genuinely changes Nadia's later possibilities. But the unresolved past must not be retrofitted so she was “always waiting” for precisely that user to solve her. Even real agreement on a symbolic association does not prove the world contains the implied shared upstream cause. The encounter can change the person without making the visitor the secret author of that person.

**The UX consequence of a single historical world is controlled access to a field of truth, not a better story generator.** The original present-world constitution distinguished public external anchors, the admitted synthetic local world and learned-world priors.
